Des Hercules C-130 dotés d’équipages ayant reçu une formation spéciale et munis de systèmes 
spécialisés d’autodéfense, de navigation à basse altitude et d’évitement du sol fourniront un soutien 
opérationnel ou à l’échelle du théâtre d’opérations =  
C-130 Hercules aircraft manned by specially trained crews flying aircraft with specialized self-defence and 
low-level navigation/terrain avoidance suites. 
 
Une composante autonome, capable de se déployer et de mener des opérations très pointues et de 
longue durée en faisant preuve d’un professionnalisme éprouvé et qui peut retourner au pays sans 
assistance après les missions. Cette composante devrait être mobilisée, formée et soutenue par des 
experts. Enfin, elle doit être contrôlée à l’échelon stratégique pour maximiser son efficacité dans l’intérêt 
national. =  
A stand-alone component fully capable of deploying itself, conducting operations at the highest level and with 
the greatest skill for extended periods of time, and returning home unassisted when the assigned task is 
completed. The resources comprising the contribution should be raised, trained and sustained by the subject 
matter experts. Lastly, control of this asset must be retained at the national strategic level to ensure its most 
effective use and employment in the national interest. 
 
 
2. Quelques observations sur les forces spéciales modernes =  
 
Observations on Modern SOF 
 
Premièrement, les forces spéciales sont réellement devenues le quatrième élément des opérations 
interarmées. Elles ne sont pas seulement un ajout, un multiplicateur de force ou une composante 
facultative; elles sont indispensables. Sans elles, une coalition aurait moins de chances d’être victorieuse. 
= First, SOF have truly become the fourth component of joint operations. They are not solely an add-on, a 
force multiplier, or an optional approach. They are a critical component to success. Without them a military 
coalition will be less likely to win. 
 
Deuxièmement, ces forces sont aujourd’hui très demandées et continueront à l’être. =  
Second, SOF are in demand today and for the future. 
 
Troisièmement, il faut des années pour monter une telle organisation et former du personnel compétent. = 
Third, it takes years to create SOF structures and to develop competent SOF personnel. 
 
Quatrièmement, utilisées à bon escient, les forces spéciales permettent de retirer des gains sur les plans 
militaire, diplomatique et politique, qui sont sans commune mesure avec leur nombre. Elles sont 
rentables. =  
Fourth, when used appropriately, SOF create military, diplomatic and political successes out of all proportion 
to their numbers. They are cost-effective. 
 
Cinquièmement, pour bien exploiter les forces antiterroristes, il faut qu’elles collaborent avec d’autres 
organismes au renseignement ou à l’amélioration du renseignement. Ces forces ne sont pas faites pour les 
opérations militaires traditionnelles et ne devraient pas en mener. Pour avoir une efficacité maximale, elles 
doivent être intégrées dans des formations homogènes et disposer de tout ce dont elles ont besoin. = 
Fifth, specialized counter-terrorist SOF are best used as a part of coordinated interagency teams, either 
conducting intelligence-led operations or conducting operations to develop intelligence. They do not and 
should not conduct conventional military operations. To be most effective, they must be established within 
coherent and ready standing formations, with all the tools necessary to function. 
 
Sixièmement, les forces spéciales sont précises, létales et distinctes. Lors des opérations préventives et 
de celles qui sont menées pendant et après un conflit, elles font partie de la solution et non du problème. 
Elles peuvent être préparées de manière à connaître la région dans laquelle elles se déploieront et à 
pouvoir communiquer avec les Autochtones; elles s’entraînent pour fonctionner et combattre dans des 
environnements complexes et sensibles. En général, les éventuels dommages collatéraux sont évalués 
en fonction des objectifs non seulement militaires mais politiques et humanitaires. =  
Sixth, SOF are precise, lethal and discriminate. In preventative, conflict and post-conflict operations, they are 
part of the solution not part of the problem. They can be structured so as to have knowledge of the 
operational locale and the language skills needed to function amongst the indigenous population, and they 
train to operate and fight in sophisticated and sensitive environments. Considerations of collateral damage 
are weighed, as a rule, not just against the military objective to be achieved, but against the political and 
humanitarian goals.

[Page 87 / 217 | chars: 5155]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Page 87 
 
Enfin, comme on l’a vu en Afghanistan et en Irak, toutes les forces spéciales ne sont pas égales. Ce serait 
une erreur de faire mener à des forces antiterroristes une guerre non traditionnelle à découvert. Les 
unités d’action directe, comme les Rangers, ne sont pas faites pour accomplir des tâches sophistiquées 
ou pour conquérir « les cœurs et les esprits ». Les pays du Groupe des Huit, dont le Canada, peuvent se 
doter de cette composante hors pair, contrairement à de nombreux autres pays. =  
Finally, as seen in both Afghanistan and Iraq, all SOF are not equal. Counter-terrorist forces should not be 
used to conduct overt unconventional warfare. Direct action units like Rangers are not suited for discriminate 
engagements or ‘hearts and minds’ tasks70. These are high-end forces that G-8 nations like Canada can 
generate, whereas many other nations cannot. 
 
 
3. Caractérisques principales = main features 
 
Un membre des forces spéciales doit être autonome, ingénieux, fortement motivé, intellectuellement 
doué, en bonne forme physique et équilibré sur le plan psychologique. Il doit rester toujours 
imperturbable, se montrer à l’aise sous la pression. Le tact et l’art de la persuasion sont également 
importants. =  
A SOF operator needs to be highly motivated, with a keen71 intellect, while being psychologically stable and 
physically fit, as well as resourceful and self-reliant. He should possess an unflappable personality, and grace 
under pressure. Tact and persuasive skills are also important. 
De par leur nature, les opérations spéciales sont discrètes. Rapides, audacieuses, basées sur le leurre et 
l’effet de surprise, elles visent à réduire les risques au minimum et à maximiser les résultats. Ces 
tactiques, ces techniques et ces méthodes permettent aux forces spéciales d’accomplir des missions que les 
forces traditionnelles pourraient souvent mener, mais plus difficilement. Ce sont donc les « forces favorites ». 
Nos alliés, notamment les États-Unis et la Grande-Bretagne, comptent parmi leurs forces spéciales des 
soldats qui connaissent bien les langues et les cultures de divers pays et peuvent ainsi s’adapter 
rapidement à des milieux très différents du nôtre et assumer leurs missions dans des délais très brefs. = 
By their very nature, SOF operations are low-visibility, utilizing speed, surprise, audacity and deception to 
minimize the associated risks, while maximizing the results. These tactics, techniques and procedures 
(TTPs) enable SOF forces to accomplish missions that, in many cases, conventional military forces could 
likely accomplish, albeit with greater difficulty. Hence, they are a “force of choice.” Our allied forces – the 
United States and Great Britain in particular – have SOF operators who are geographically oriented, and, 
therefore, culturally attuned and capable of communicating in the local language(s). This capability enables 
them to acclimatize rapidly in exotic locales and undertake their missions from a standing start72. 
 
Les moyens d’insertion ou d’exfiltration sont nombreux : parachutes, propulseurs sous-marins, 
bateaux, sous-marins, hélicoptères, appareils de plongée sous-marine, kayaks à deux places de type 
KlepperMC, etc. Étant donné que les populations du globe quittent les campagnes pour vivre dans des centres 
urbains, il faudra sans doute utiliser des techniques moins « james-bondiennes ». Les forces spéciales 
doivent apprendre à se fondre dans la population, au pays et à l’étranger, tout en étant entièrement 
équipées pour réaliser leur mission. Elles doivent acquérir les techniques des opérations clandestines afin 
de s’approcher de leur cible sans se faire remarquer ou, comme on dit dans le Special Air Service, d’une 
manière keeni meeni73 et de quitter rapidement le secteur sans laisser de trace. =  
Parachute, swimmer delivery vehicles (SDV), boat, submarine, helicopter, scuba and Klepper™ (two man 
kayak) operations are just some of the means available for insertion or extraction. As global populations 
move from rural to urban centres, SOF forces might have to seek out ‘lower testosterone-driven techniques’ 
of infiltration and extraction. They must learn to blend into domestic and foreign populations, while fully 
equipped to undertake the mission at hand. This requires training in clandestine tradecraft, so that operators 
might move innocuously, or, as the SAS would say, in a keeni meeni fashion toward their objective, and, after 
the mission is completed, egress the area expeditiously without a trace. 
 
 
 
 
                                                          
70 Gagner les cœurs et les esprits = to win hearts and minds.

73 En swahili, cette expression décrit le mouvement sinueux d’un serpent venimeux dans l’herbe haute et 
désigne un travail clandestin très dangereux. = Keeni meeni is a Swahili phrase to denote extremely 
dangerous undercover work. It refers to the sinuous movement of a deadly snake in long grass.

3. VOCABULAIRE AIR 
 
 
The CFAS (airborne component of nuclear deterrence) and the CDAOA (missions related to the air defence 
role) are part of the operational chain of command. 
 
The CFAC (offensive and defensive force which ensures the defence of French national airspace), CFAP 
(rapid and long range projection of forces to defend our country and protect its interests throughout the 
world), CASSIC (possesses the surveillance and control [national early warning], ground-to-air defence 
[defence of air bases that have a significant role in the airborne nuclear force, mainly] and electronic support 
assets), CEAA (initial and further training of personnel throughout their careers) and CFPSAA (defence of 
sensitive Air Force sites) are part of the organic chain of command. 
 
The territorial chain of command includes two Air Regions (North and South) in which Air Force bases are 
located.

Network-Centric Warfare focuses “on the greater 
synergistic effect that can be created by networking and 
electronically linking geographically separated forces into 
one sensor-to-shooter engagement grid.” It provides the 
operator with enhanced situational and battle space 
awareness by extending the individual teams and 
network, allowing for access to a common operating 
Information Center, thus amassing information instead of 
combat forces, to effectively project combat power.

L A  L U T T E  A N T I - C H A R  
The anti-tank barrier 
 
 
La trame antichar peut se définir comme la 
combinaison sur le terrain d’un ensemble 
de moyens complémentaires donnant à 
l’infanterie, en particulier, la capacité de 
détruire les chars adverses par toute 
visibilité. 
Elle 
se 
définit 
par 
les 
performances 
techniques 
de 
ses 
constituants et par leur possibilité et leur 
niveau d’emploi opérationnel  
 the anti-tank barrier can be defined as the 
tactical 
combination 
of 
complementary 
means which provide the infantry in 
particular with the ability to destroy enemy 
tanks in all-visibility conditions. It could be 
characterized by the technical capabilities 
of its components and their capacities and 
their levels of operational use. 
Un consensus se dégage au sein des 
différentes 
armées 
de 
l’OTAN 
pour 
organiser cette trame en trois catégories de 
matériels : 
 a consensus can be drawn within NATO’s 
various armed forces to organize this 
barrier in three categories of weapons : 
- 
Les matériels de longue portée, 
armements puissants, montés sur 
véhicules blindés ou sur hélicoptères, 
qui permettent de mener le combat 
au niveau de la grande unité. La 
portée de ce type d’armement est 
typiquement de 4.000 m  
- 
the long-range, powerful, armoured 
vehicles 
or 
helicopters 
mounted 
equipment which enables the major 
units to carry out their missions. The 
typical 
range 
of 
such 
weapon 
systems is 4,000 m (2½ miles) 
- 
Les matériels de moyenne portée, 
armements 
portables, 
éventuellement adaptés ou intégrés 
sur véhicules, apportant l’allonge 
nécessaire pour mener le combat au 
niveau de la compagnie et du 
régiment. La portée de ce type 
d’armement est de 2.000 m  
- 
the medium-range, man portable 
equipment, 
possibly 
adapted 
or 
integrated to vehicles which gives the 
required range to fight at company 
and battalion levels. This kind of 
weapon can range up to 2,000 
meters (1¼ mile) 
- 
Les matériels de courte portée, 
armes 
collectives 
portatives, 
diffusées au niveau de la section ou 
du groupe de combat. Leur portée 
varie de 200 à 600 m, voire 800 m  
- 
the short-range crew-served portable 
equipment, fielded at platoon or 
squad level. Their ranges vary from 
200 m up to 600 m, even 800 m (1/8 
up to 3/8, even 1/2 mile)

L A  G U E R R E  É L E C T R O N I Q U E  
Electronic warfare 
 
 
La surveillance électronique  
 electronic surveillance 
Un radar de surveillance au sol  
 a ground-surveillance radar (GSR) 
Des moyens de surveillance  
 surveillance means 
Il complète d’autres moyens et détecte de 
façon précise des objets lorsque d’autres 
moyens ne le peuvent pas  
 it complements other means and detects 
objects accurately when others cannot 
Le radar de surveillance au sol apporte une 
capacité 
mobile 
et 
tout 
temps 
de 
surveillance du champ de bataille  
 GSR equipment provides a mobile, all-
weather capability for battlefield surveillance 
Le radar est utilisé pour : 
Radar is used to : 
- rechercher les axes de progression, les 
positions d’attaques ennemies possibles, 
les zones de regroupement ou d’autres 
zones, afin de déterminer la localisation, le 
volume, la composition et la nature de 
l’activité ennemie. Cela peut être effectué 
selon une planification, de façon aléatoire 
ou de manière permanente ; 
- search avenues of approach, possible 
enemy attack positions, assembly areas, or 
other sectors to determine the location, 
size, composition and nature of enemy 
activity. This can be done on a time 
schedule, at random, or continuously ; 
- suivre les objectifs ponctuels tels que les 
ponts, les points de passage obligés ou les 
carrefours routiers ; 
- monitor point targets such as bridges, 
defiles or road junctions ; 
- élargir l’observation ; 
- extend observation ; 
- 
détecter 
initialement 
des 
objectifs 
éloignés, partiellement masqués et flous ; 
- initially detect partially obscured (hazy) 
targets at long ranges ; 
- aider au contrôle de l’unité lors des 
opérations 
par 
visibilité 
réduite, 
en 
surveillant les têtes de colonnes  
- aid unit control during limited visibility 
operations by monitoring course headings. 
Contre-mesure électronique  
 electronic countermeasure 
Brouillage électronique  
 electronic jamming

Le Lynx Mk9 est le taxi du champ de

 the Lynx Mk9 is the battlefield taxi for the

Ses missions comprennent la mise en place

d’unités

troops,

Une mise en place type peut nécessiter une

déplaceront de la ZDA de la brigade, 
parcourront 120 kilomètres jusqu’à la ligne

l’arrivée 
de 
renforts 
supplémentaires

 a typical insertion may require a flight of 
five Lynx LBHs86, each carrying six Milan-
equipped troops, to fly from the brigade’s 
staging area, 75 miles forward to the 
frontline, and deploy 30 troops as a 
blocking force until more reinforcements 
arrive by Chinook and Puma 
Les hélicoptères87 légers du champs de

de

d’EVASAN 
et 
ravitaillement des positions avancées. Ils

basse altitude”) bien en dessous de 25 m 
(75 pieds)  
 light battlefield helicopters (LBHs) are also 
used for command and control duties, 
casevac and resupply of forward positions. 
They can operate nap-of-the-earth at well 
below 75 ft

L ’ A R M E  B L I N D É E  
Armoured Corps 
 
Un certain nombre de systèmes de blindage 
ne sont pas suffisants pour faire face à la 
toute dernière menace  
 many armour systems are not sufficient to 
meet the latest threat 
Cela 
concerne 
particulièrement 
les 
véhicules blindés légers à roues et à 
chenilles dont le blindage n’offre de 
protection que contre les armes de petit 
calibre, 7.62 et 12.7 mm  
 this applies particularly to light tracked and 
wheeled armoured vehicles whose armour 
gives protection only against 7.62 mm or 
12.7 mm small arms 
La menace attachée aux armes d’un calibre 
plus 
important 
est 
particulièrement 
inquiétante dans certaines opérations de 
maintien de la paix. Par exemple le lance 
grenade antichar de la famille RPG-7, 
matériel russe largement répandu, qui tire 
une grenade propulsée par roquette et qui 
est doté d’une charge militaire de type 
HEAT (high explosive anti-tank), peut 
perforer n’importe quel véhicule blindé léger 
et même certains chars  
 the threat from larger calibre weapons is of 
particular concern in some peacekeeping 
operations. 
For 
example, 
the 
widely 
deployed Russian RPG-7 series anti-tank 
grenade launcher, which fires a rocket 
propelled grenade with a HEAT warhead, 
can penetrate any light armoured vehicle 
and even some MBT’s88 
Le développement, la production et la mise 
en dotation de systèmes de blindage 
additionnel nécessitent un haut savoir-faire 
spécialisé pour mettre au point un système 
qui fournira le niveau de protection requis 
tout 
en 
satisfaisant 
d’autres 
besoins 
opérationnels  
 the development, production and fielding of 
add-on armour packages require a high 
degree of specialization in key arts to 
develop a package that will provide the 
required protection level and also satisfy 
other operational needs 
La division armements au sein de la 
branche militaire de British Aerospace a 
divulgué 
des 
détails 
supplémentaires 
relatifs au développement sur fonds propres 
du système de blindage ROMOR qu’il 
commercialise auprès d’un certain nombre 
de clients à l’étranger  
 British 
Aerospace 
Defence’s 
Royal 
Ordnance Division has released additional 
details of its private venture ROMOR 
armour system which it is marketing to a 
number of overseas customers 
Le système ROMOR-A comprend deux 
parties : les briquettes et le système de 
fixation. 
Ce 
dernier 
est 
normalement 
boulonné ou soudé sur le véhicule et les 
briquettes de blindage explosif réactif sont 
fixées à demeure à l’aide de boulons. Les 
briquettes 
consistent 
en 
une 
couche 
d’explosif 
prise 
entre 
deux 
plaques. 
Lorsqu’il est touché par le projectile, 
l’explosif sépare les deux plaques et détruit 
le dard de métal que la charge creuse dirige 
vers l’objectif  
 ROMOR-A consists of two parts : the tiles 
and the mounting system. The latter is 
normally bolted or welded onto the vehicle 
and the ERA89 tiles are bolted in place. The 
tiles consist of a layer of explosive 
sandwiched between two plates. When hit 
by a projectile the explosive drives the two 
plates apart and breaks up the jet of metal 
which the shaped charge directs at the 
target 
Un véhicule d’observation de l’artillerie  an OP vehicle (GB), a FISTV (US) 
                                                          
88 Main Battle Tank. 
89 Explosive Reactive Armour.

 such a force should be organized as a light

Il

J’étais

 I was a new second lieutenant starting the

Je faisais équipe avec trois autres sous-

 I was teamed with three other second

Comme stagiaires, nous allions apprendre à

 as trainees we would learn each crew 
position in the tank : driver, loader, gunner 
and tank commander 
Monter sur des destriers d’acier  
 to mount iron steeds 
Ayant appris à nous salir, à nous enduire de 
graisse et à nous fatiguer à force de nous 
consacrer à l’entretien du char, vint l’étape 
suivante tant attendue : le pas de tir  
 the first step after learning how to get dirty, 
greasy and tired maintaining the tank was 
the much anticipated gunnery range 
Le premier jour au pas de tir, j’étais plein 
d’enthousiasme à l’idée d’être en mesure 
de 
tenir 
chacune 
des 
fonctions 
de 
l’équipage  
 on the first day at the range I was excited 
to be able to perform from each crew 
position 
Je fus d’abord chef de char. Je sortis ma 
tête par l’écoutille afin de regarder le tir du 
char suivant et fus assommé par la 
déflagration  
 first I was the tank commander. I pocked 
my head out of the hatch to watch the next 
tank fire and was knocked silly by the 
concussion 
Puis je devins tireur. Je collai mon œil au 
système de visée et pressai la détente pour 
tirer  
 next I got to be the gunner. I put my eye on 
the sight and squeezed the trigger to fire 
Il ne m’était jamais venu à l’esprit qu’un 
char de 60 tonnes puisse se cabrer et me 
frapper en pleine figure, mais il le fit  
 it never occurred to me that a 60-ton tank 
could rock back and smash me in the face, 
but it did 
Ensuite, comme radio-chargeur, je fis du 
bon boulot, car nous tirâmes une série de 
dix obus en un temps très court  
 next as the loader, I did a good job as we 
fired 10 rounds in quick succession 
Cependant, je me rendis compte que je me 
tenais sur des douilles d’obus brûlantes et 
que les semelles de mes rangers étaient en 
train de fondre  
 however I discovered that I was standing 
on hot shell casings, and my (combat) boot 
soles were melting.

L A  L O G I S T I Q U E  
Logistics 
 
Les attributions logistiques de la division  
 Division logistic functions 
La division prévoit et organise, en fonction de sa 
manœuvre, le soutien logistique de ses formations 
organiques et, selon les ordres du CA, celui des 
formations qui lui sont adaptées ou données en 
renforcement  
 the Division level plans and organises, according to 
the manoeuvre, logistic support to all organic units 
and, depending on the Corps level orders, to 
assigned or attached units 
Le ravitaillement  
 resupply function 
Maintien en condition : elle assure le maintien en 
condition des matériels de ses formations ; la division 
organise, en outre, les mouvements, les transports et 
la circulation dans sa zone  
 maintenance function : it maintains the equipment of 
its units. The Division level furthermore organises the 
road movements, transport and traffic control in its 
area of responsibility 
Santé : elle amorce les évacuations primaires à l’aide 
des véhicules sanitaires qui lui sont détachés par le 
CA. Elle participe au traitement des blessés les plus 
urgents  
 medical function : it initiates primary evacuations 
with the front line ambulances allocated by the Corps 
level and participates in extreme urgency wounded 
treating 
Soutien de l’homme : cette fonction regroupe 
l’administration et les soutiens divers (CAT, poste 
aux armées, trésorerie aux armées, aumôneries)  
 soldier’s support : it covers administration and 
various support services such as quartermaster, 
mail, finance support and chaplain

money is the sinews of war

 Warrior section vehicles carry driver, commander,

93 
The 2015 armed forces model 
 
 
Les options stratégiques qui ont orienté les 
grandes caractéristiques de ce nouveau 
modèle d’armée privilégient les fonctions de 
prévention et de projection des forces, la 
dissuasion étant maintenue au niveau de 
suffisance  
 the strategic options which have oriented 
the main characteristics of this new model 
armed forces favour prevention and forces 
projectioning functions ; the deterrence 
component is maintained at adequacy 
threshold 
La prévention implique le développement du 
renseignement, surtout d’origine spatiale, et 
le prépositionnement de forces  
 prevention implies the development of 
intelligence, especially SAT-INT, as well as 
the prepositioning of forces 
L’accent mis sur la projection amène à 
concevoir des forces armées ramassées, 
disponibles 
avec 
de 
faibles 
préavis, 
mobiles, polyvalentes, dotées de matériels 
modernes leur donnant dès le départ la 
supériorité tactique  
 the emphasis (or “stress”) put on the 
projectioning leads (or “conducts”) to the 
conception of compact, mobile, multi-
purpose armed forces, available on short 
notice, and fielded with modern equipment, 
providing them, from the very beginning, 
with tactical superiority 
C’est l’armée de terre qui subit les plus 
importantes transformations  
the Army will undergo the most important 
changes 
Passant à un format de 170 000 hommes, 
elle adoptera une nouvelle organisation 
pour réduire le nombre de ses états-majors 
et ses organismes de soutien et se 
concentrer sur ses fonctions opérationnelles  
with a strength downsized to 170 000 men 
(or “troops”), it will adopt a new organization 
to reduce the number of its HQs and 
support formations and focus on (or “ 
concentrate on”) its operational tasks (or 
“missions”) 
L’armée de terre s’articulera désormais 
autour d’une Force blindée, d’une Force 
mécanisée, 
d’une 
Force 
d’intervention 
blindée rapide et d’une Force d’infanterie 
d’assaut  
 the Army will henceforth be articulated 
around an armoured force, a mechanized 
force, a rapid intervention force and an 
infantry assault force

94 1MC = One Main Circuit, is the term for the shipboard public address circuits on naval vessels of the 
United States. This provides a means of transmitting general information and orders to all internal ship 
spaces and topside areas, and is loud enough that all embarked personnel are able to hear it. 
Usage examples : 
« General Quarters* ! General Quarters ! All hands [= tous les hommes**] man your battle stations ! » 
« Reveille ! Reveille ! Reveille ! All hands heave out and trice up ! [= Branle-bas !] Reveille ! » 
« Taps ! Taps ! Lights out ! Taps ! » 
« Darken ship ! Make Darken Ship reports to CCS*** » 
« Flight Quarters**** ! Flight Quarters ! Set condition 1-alpha for flight operations ! All personnel remove soft 
hats ! All hands man your Flight Quarters stations !» 
« The following is a test of the helo crash alarm from the flight deck – disregard this alarm. Test complete – 
regard all further alarms ! » 
 
* “General Quarters !” (US Navy) = “Action Stations ! ” (Royal Navy) = Aux postes de combat ! (autrefois 
“branle-bas de combat !”). 
** She was lost with all hands = il a coulé avec tous les hommes à bord / tout l'équipage. (“She” est employé 
pour souligner l’affection et le respect que portent les marins à leur bateau). 
*** Central control station. 
**** “Flight Quarters !” = a ship configuration that assigns and stations personnel at critical positions to 
conduct safe flight operations. 
95 Life saving procedure for sweeping for IEDs using visual checks for ground sign and metal detectors. 
Op Barma = clearing mines and IEDs in front of vehicles or foot patrols.  
To “OP BAR(A)MA” means to make sure there are no secondary explosive devices (after IED-Strike or Find). 
96 A police dog, often referred to as a "K-9 dog" in some areas (which is a homophone of “canine”).

[Page 117 / 217 | chars: 3179]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Page 117 
E N G L I S H  M A R I T I M E  P H R A S E S  
 
 
 to the bitter end : jusqu’au bout. Ex: We are determined to hold on to this project to the bitter end. 
 
 to miss the boat : s’utilise pour parler d’une occasion manquée. 
Ex : He’s missed the boat. He’ll never get another opportunity like that. 
 
 to be in the same boat : être dans le même bateau. S’utilise pour parler d’une situation où tous les 
protagonistes sont concernés de façon similaire. 
Ex :You’re not the only one to suffer. We’re all in the same boat. 
 
 not room enough to swing a cat : s’utilise pour parler d’un espace limité, et toujours avec une 
connotation négative. Ex :The room was so tiny that there was not room enough to swing a cat. 
 
 to show one’s true colours : expression qui implique la tromperie, l’hypocrisie, que l’on montre son 
vrai visage. Ex : He showed his true colours when he let his friend down. 
 
 with flying colours : Peux se traduire par “haut la main”, quand qqun sort d’une situation avec 
succès. Ex : He emerged from his exam with flying colours. 
 
 on the crest of a wave : au sommet du succès. 
Ex : The company is on the crest of a wave and has just announced record profits. 
 
 out of one’s depth : s’utilise pour dire que qqun fait qqch qui excède ses capacités. 
Ex : He’s out of his depth in his new job. 
 
 at a low ebb : la marée basse reflète ici les sentiments ou la situation de qqun. 
Ex : He’s just been dumped by his girlfriend. His spirits are at a low ebb. 
 
 to be left high and dry : fait référence a un bateau qui échoue sur la terre ferme. 
S’emploie quand on est dans une situation où l’on pas les ressources (souvent financières) pour 
s’en sortir. Ex : When the airplanes’ strike was announced, the travellers were left high and dry. 
 
 to leave little/no room for manoeuvre : cette métaphore s’emploie dans tous types de situations pour 
exprimer le fait que l’on n’a plus beaucoup d’options. 
Ex : The breakdown in negotiations leaves little room for manoeuvre. 
 
 a drop in the ocean : exprime le fait qu’une chose est complètement inadéquate par rapport à une 
situation. Ex : The amount of money devoted to this unemployment problem is a drop in the ocean. 
 
 to rock the boat : se réfère au tangage et au roulis et au risque qu’ils impliquent pour un bateau. 
S’emploie pour dire q’un ou des individus mettent en péril les intérêts d’une organisation à laquelle 
ils appartiennent. 
Ex : In order to keep the battalion united, the Sergeant asked the young enlistees not to rock the 
boat. 
 
 to hit rock bottom : toucher le fond. Ex : When the father lost his job, the family hit rock bottom. 
 
 plain sailing : facile, pas compliqué. Ex : The exam was plain sailing for him. 
 
 spick and span : s’utilisait en langage marine pour parler d’un bateau tout neuf. 
S’utilise pour dire de qqch que c’est en ordre, neuf et propre. 
Ex : In the barracks, everything looks spick and span for the daily inspection. 
 
 in dire straits : S’utilise pour parler d’une situation dont on a peu de chance de se tirer rapidement. 
Ex : They suddenly found themselves in dire straits, with no job and nowhere to live.

M I L I T A R Y ,  N A V A L  A N D  A I R  
A C A D E M I E S  
 
Perhaps the first country to develop a comprehensive [=complet] and modernly 
efficient scheme [=système] of military education was Prussia, which had such early brilliant 
reformers as Gerhard von Scharnhorst, August von Gneisenau, and Carl von Clausewitz 
and whose complex of military institutions in the 19th century would elicit [=obtenir, provoquer] 
the respect (and often the imitation) of other military powers. At the base of the officer-
training system were eventually 8 cadet schools, more or less for the upper class or elite, 
and 10 war schools for the less select [=privilégiés] - both training men for commission [=to 
get one's commission, to be commissioned (as) an officer = être nommé officier]. At the apex [=point 
culminant, sommet] of the system was the venerable War Academy, or Kriegs Akademie, at 
Berlin, founded in 1810 and offering the highest advanced education for commissioned 
officers. A great complex of technical and auxiliary schools, such as for cavalry and 
engineering, filled in [=constituer] the system. After World War I the entire complex was 
disrupted [=interrompre], though the military tradition persisted. 
In France the system developed in simpler form, with two traditional channels 
[=voies] for men seeking commissions. Military engineers, artillerymen, and other technical 
officers were trained at the École Polytechnique, an engineering school that was 
founded in 1794. Staff and other officers for the infantry and cavalry were trained at the 
École Spéciale Militaire de Saint-Cyr, founded by Napoleon in 1803. The École Navale 
at Brest trains officers for the navy, and the École de l'Air at Salon-de-Provence is the air 
force academy. 
Until 1939 potential officers for the British army were trained at either the Royal 
Military Academy at Woolwich (founded 1741), offering training in artillery, engineering, 
and communications, or the Royal Military College at Sandhurst (founded 1802), offering 
cavalry and infantry training. From 1940 the functions of these schools were combined at 
Sandhurst, which in 1947 was officially designated the Royal Military Academy, 
Sandhurst. British naval cadets are enrolled at the Royal Naval College, Dartmouth; and 
air force cadets train at the Royal Air Force College, Cranwell (founded 1920). Selected 
commissioned officers are educated in higher strategy and policy at the Imperial Defence 
College. 
The United States has four major academies: the United States Military 
Academy at West Point, N.Y. (founded 1802); the United States Naval Academy at 
Annapolis, Md. (founded 1845); the United States Air Force Academy at Colorado 
Springs, Colo. (founded 1954); and the United States Coast Guard Academy at New 
London, Conn. (founded 1876). There is also the United States Merchant Marine 
Academy at Kings Point, N.Y. (founded 1943), whose graduates [=diplômés] may apply for 
commissions in the naval reserve. At the apex of the system, for training commissioned 
officers of the three basic services [=armées] in higher strategy and policy, are the National 
War College and the Industrial College of the Armed Forces, the latter for study of 
economic aspects of mobilization. Advanced training is also offered at the several 
[=différentes] academies and at non-military universities. 
 
Source : Encyclopædia Britannica.

Le monogramme royal  = royal cypher  
 
La reine Elizabeth II = Elizabeth II Regina 
 
 
 
 
Le monogramme royal est le monogramme personnel du souverain du Royaume-Uni  
= The royal cypher is the UK sovereign’s monogram. 
 
Le monogramme de la reine est « E II R », représentant l’initiale du nom de la reine et de son 
titre « Regina », signifiant « reine », surmonté d’une couronne.  
= The Queen’s cypher is « E II R », consisting of the initials of the monarch's name and title  
«  Regina », which means « Queen » surmounted by a crown.

B R I T I S H  F O R C E S  
 
 
The Sovereign, as the head of state, is technically in charge of all the Armed Forces.  
In reality, Parliament is the decision-making body for defence. The Cabinet, and in particular 
the Secretary of State for Defence, is responsible to Parliament for these matters.  
The day-to-day running of the Armed Forces - in both peacetime and wartime - is the 
responsibility of the Defence Council, which is made up of members of Ministry of Defence and 
the Armed Forces. 
 
 
HM FORCES PERSONNEL 2012

In an emergency, the UK MoD could call upon a tri-service reserve component of some 216,000 personnel

[Page 137 / 217 | chars: 2043]

Page 137 
 
The Armed Forces 
 
Questions & Answers 
 
 
 
How old do I have to be to join the Services?  
Officers 17 years, Other Ranks 16 years. 
 
What sort of qualifications do you need? 
Officers: 2 GCE 'A' Levels (or equivalent), and 5 GCSEs (or equivalent) with English & Maths 
as mandatory subjects. Other Ranks need to pass a recruit selection test. 
 
Can women be fighter pilots? 
Yes. There are already female fixed wing pilots106 in the RAF. 
 
I have heard that women can’t join the Infantry. Why? 
All cap-badged posts107 in the Regular Infantry [= tous les postes de combattants dans 
l’infanterie] (and Household Cavalry/Royal Armoured Corps) are presently closed to women on 
the grounds of combat effectiveness. The Government is wholly committed to maximising 
employment opportunities for women in the Armed Forces and, to this end the Army is 
currently conducting a study into the effects of gender upon combat effectiveness, which is 
due to report to Ministers in 2001. 
 
How many women and people from ethnic minorities are already employed in the 
Services? 
At 1 October 1999 (latest figures available) there were 2,281 ethnic minority personnel in the 
Armed Forces, representing 1.1% of the total strength. 
At 1 October 1999 (latest figures available) there were 16,454 women in the Armed Forces, 
representing 7.9% of the total strength. 
 
 
 
 
 
                                                          
106 Fixed-wing pilots are pilots who fly fixed-wing aircraft. Aircraft are either fixed-wing (such as Jumbo jet, 
Concorde or Harrier [Harrier jump jet]) or rotary-wing (helicopters). 
 
107 Cap-badged posts are a means of identifying people from certain regiments and corps in the British Army to fill 
certain jobs. If you want someone who look after administrative jobs, then the post they fill will be cap-badged for a 
member of the Adjutant general’s Corps. Similarly, if you want someone who knows about firing guns and 
cannons, then the post will be for a member of the Royal Artillery.

113 FLY-PAST AVRO LANCASTER 
On 4 May 09, a Lancaster aircraft from the UK will overfly Brunssum as part of the Memorial Day commemoration.  
Weather permitting, the flypast will occur at 1400 hours over the Lancaster memorial at Platanendreef 42 in 
Brunssum (near where the road from Nuth passes over the N276 to Sittard).  This will be a marvellous opportunity 
to see one of the 2 remaining flying Lancaster aircraft make a rare overflight in The Netherlands and all JFCB 
personnel are invited to watch the flypast.  The Dutch authorities have been informed of the flypast and there is 
likely to be significant interest from the local Dutch population.  Our DCOM will be in attendance and JFCB 
personnel are encouraged to attend this event in uniform.  
David R P. - Gp Capt UK AF

P A R A D E  P R E P A R A T I O N  
 
 
Uniform will be BDU with Combat Jacket and Gloves. 
Transportation by bus. 
 
The group of Off’s and NCO’s are called separately  ”guard”. 
I will have the function of  “Guard Commander"  NCO’s. 
 
Each command at the parade will start with the warning command = “Guard !” 
For example: 
 
Guard - Attention 
Guard - Stand at ease 
Guard - Right /Left Dress (only heads turn, move till elbow distance) 
Guard - Eyes front 
Guard - Dismiss (turn right, start walking) 
 
Commands can be given by: 
Parade Commander = Gen Steltz 
Parade SM = CSM Oullette 
Guard Cdr  = OF4 (tbd) and me 
 
At the commands: Present Arms, Shoulder Arms, only the guard commanders and the colour 
parties will response. 
The guards (Off and NCO) remain in ‘Attention” position. 
 
General remarks: 
The gathering for the bus will be at 08.00.  
First a check if everybody is there, then departure by bus.  
With a little luck, coffee should be available during the training/rehearsal.  
If training goes well, it does not have to take as long as on the timetable. 
The parade itself will take about 2 hours, from 10.00 till 12.00. 
More details to be given by CSM Oullette. 
If you have any questions, please contact me. 
 
Thank you. 
 
Jos S. 
AOO OR-9

W R I T E  O U T  Y O U R  R E S U M E 
119
 
Rédigez votre C.V. 
 
 
 
Chief of the Air Staff 
 
Air Chief Marshal Sir Jock Stirrup KCB AFC ADC FRAeS FCMI 
RAF (appointed 01 August 2003) 
 
Air Chief Marshal Stirrup was educated at Merchant Taylors’ 
School, Northwood and the Royal Air Force College Cranwell, and was commissioned in 1970. 
After a tour as a Qualified Flying Instructor he served on loan with the Sultan of Oman’s Air 
Force, operating Strikemasters in the Dhofar War. Returning to the United Kingdom in 1975 he 
was posted to No 41(F) Squadron, flying Jaguars in the Fighter Reconnaissance role, before 
taking up an exchange appointment on RF-4C Phantoms in the United States. He then spent 
two years at Lossiemouth as a flight commander on the Jaguar Operational Conversion Unit, 
and subsequently attended the Joint Service Defence College in 1984. He commanded No 
II(AC) Squadron, flying Fighter Reconnaissance Jaguars from Royal Air Force Laarbruch, until 
1987 when he took up the post of Personal Staff Officer to the Chief of the Air Staff. He 
assumed command of Royal Air Force Marham in 1990, just in time for Operation GRANBY, 
and then attended the 1993 Course at the Royal College of Defence Studies. He completed No 
7 Higher Command and Staff Course at Camberley prior to becoming the Director of Air Force 
Plans and Programmes in 1994. He became Air Officer Commanding No 1 Group in April 1997 
and was appointed Assistant Chief of the Air Staff in August 1998. 
 
He took up the appointment of Deputy Commander-in-Chief Strike Command in 2000. At the 
same time he assumed the additional roles of Commander of NATO’s Combined Air Operations 
Centre 9 and Director of the European Air Group. He spent the last few months of his tour, from 
September 2001 to January 2002, as UK National Contingent Commander and Senior British 
Military Advisor to CINCUSCENTCOM for Operation VERITAS, the UK’s contribution to the 
United States led Operation ENDURING FREEDOM in Afghanistan. Air Chief Marshal Stirrup 
was appointed KCB in the New Year Honours List 2002 and became Deputy Chief of the 
Defence Staff (Equipment Capability) in March 2002. He was appointed Chief of the Air Staff, 
on promotion, on 1 August 2003. 
 
Air Chief Marshal Stirrup is married with one son and enjoys golf, music, theatre and history. 
 
Last Updated: 26 Aug 04

Deputy Director March 2004 - 
 
FRENCH AIR FORCE 
GENERAL DE BRIGADE AERIENNE MARTIAL IMBERTI 
 
Brigadier General Martial IMBERTI was born on 5th August 1952 at Troyes. 
He entered the French Air Force Academy at Salon de Provence in 
September 1972, and gained his fighter pilot wings in 1976.  
Posted to Luxeuil Air Force base, he gained his first operational experience 
flying the Mirage III E aircraft in the Lafayette squadron, and became Flight 
Commander in 1981. 
In 1983, as 1st solo in the French aerobatic team, “la Patrouille de France”, 
he represented France and the French Air Force with aerobatic displays over 
Europe. Commandant Martial IMBERTI was appointed commander and 
leader of this well known flying unit in 1984. 
In 1985 he joined the “Normandie-Niemen” squadron, flying the Mirage F1 
jet fighter and taking part in an operational commitment in Chad. He became 
commander of the squadron in 1986. 
He was posted to the 2nd Fighter Wing at Dijon Air Force base in May 1987. The Wing was equipped with the 
Mirage 2000C, Air Defence aircraft. Lieutenant Colonel IMBERTI performed the duties of Chief of Operations, 
and Commander of the 2nd Fighter Wing. During this time, he led the Mirage 2000C presentation team to help 
promote export sales in the Middle East and in Asia.  
In 1990 he was selected to attend the Air Warfare and Joint Defence College, and moved to Metz as Chief of 
Plans and Programs at the French Combat Air Force. There, he participated in two Red Flag exercises and in 
further international air shows. In addition, he participated to NATO air staff in Italy in charge of air operations in 
the Balkans. Then he commanded the French air forces during the Operation “Turquoise“, which played a key 
role in the operatios and the delivery of humanitarian relief following the conflict in Rwanda in 1994. Due to his 
experience and knowledge of operations in Africa, in 1995 he was given command of the French air base in 
Djibouti for two years. 
After a very successful operational career, Colonel IMBERTI attended the Institute of Advanced Studies for 
Defence in Paris, and completed a variety of high level staff and command posts. In 1998, he was entrusted with 
the post of Chief of Staff of “l’Inspection Générale des Armées Air“ depending on the Minister of Defence. 
 On 1st July 2000, he was promoted to Brigadier General, and appointed Chief of Division within the Department 
of International Affairs in the Joint Defence Staff in Paris. In October 2001, he attended the NATO General Flag 
Officer Course in Rome and Brussels. He represented the French Ministry of Defence on Afghanistan security 
during the G8 Geneva and Paris summits in 2002. 
Brigadier General IMBERTI was appointed Deputy Commander of the Air Defence and Air Operations 
Command at Taverny on 1st September 2002. As such, he was the French representative to the NATO Air 
Defence Committee and the NATO ACCS Management Organisation (NACMO) Board. He also represented the 
French Air Force on the management Committee of the 2003 Paris Air Show. 
He was appointed Deputy Director of the European Air Group on 1st April 2004, RAF High Wycombe, and 
attended the NATO Senior Officers Policy Course in 2005. 
Brigadier General IMBERTI is an Officier of "la Légion d'Honneur", Commander of "l'Ordre national du mérite", 
holder of "la médaille de l'Aéronautique" and of "La Croix de la valeur militaire". 
He has 5200 hours of flying experience of which 3800 are in fighter aircraft of various types. His combat 
experience includes 40 war missions. 
Martial IMBERTI is married to Sophie. They have six children and four grandchildren.  
Source : http://www.euroairgroup.org/deputy_cv.htm

[Page 149 / 217 | chars: 3037]

Page 149 
 
Chief Of Staff 
 
ROYAL AIR FORCE 
GROUP CAPTAIN D J BLORE MSc BSc MRAeS RAF 
 
Group Captain David Blore joined the Royal Air Force as a University Cadet 
in the Supply Branch in 1978. He read120 Nuclear Engineering at Manchester 
University and graduated in 1981 with 1st Class Honours. His first tour was 
at 30MU Sealand responsible for the provision of support to the Phantom 
and Tornado aircraft. A change of career direction followed in 1984 when he 
completed the Officer’s Movements Course and was subsequently posted to 
Brize Norton as a Duty Air Movements Officer with responsibility for all 
passenger and cargo handling aspects of VC10 and Tristar aircraft 
movements. 
A 4 months detachment in the Falkland Islands as OC Air Movements Flight 
was followed by 3 years in HQ RAF Germany as a staff officer in the 
Command Movements Branch. In 1989, he was promoted to squadron 
leader and joined an IT systems design team at the RAF Logistics 
Establishment. However, this appointment was curtailed121 with a 6 month 
tour in HQ British Forces Middle East where he had responsibility for tasking 
RAF C-130 aircraft and their associated air movements support organisation 
within the Arabian Peninsula during the first Gulf War. 
On return to the UK he completed a Masters degree in Transportation Studies at Cranfield Institute of 
Technology before moving to the Ministry of Defence in 1992. Half way through his tour, where he was 
responsible for the future policy and plans for personnel, air cargo handling equipment and IT of the air 
movements organisation in peace and crisis he was promoted to wing commander. A year at the RAF Staff 
College, where he received the British Aerospace Prize, preceded his appointment as OC United Kingdom 
Mobile Air Movements Squadron. Subsequently, he was selected as the first non-aircrew Personal Staff Officer 
to Commander-in-chief, Strike Command, serving 3 Commander-in-chiefs from December 1998 to August 2000, 
with promotion to group captain in 2000. Thereafter, he completed 2 years in HQ Defence Logistics 
Organisation, London firstly as the movements specialist but later as the logistics policy focus, before attending 
Royal College of Defence Studies in 2003. He completed a short tour as Assistant Director International in 
Directorate of Air Staff with responsibility for the development of RAF relationships with all other air forces prior 
to his appointment as Chief of Staff, European Air Group in September 2004. 
Group Captain Blore is a keen athlete and represents the Combined Services at cross country and the RAF on 
the road. He is Chairman of the Combined Services and RAF Athletics Associations. He is also a member of the 
editorial board for the RAF’s Air Power Review. He and his wife, Sharon, have 4 children: Charlotte (1987); 
James (1989); and, twins Thomas and Matthew (2001). 
 
Source : http://www.euroairgroup.org/cos_cv.htm

F O R M S  O F  A D D R E S S  
(Formules pour s'adresser à quelqu’un) 
 
 
 
 
1. Speaking about someone 
 
 
he's a colonel 
 
 
= il est colonel 
to be promoted to colonel  
= être promu colonel 
What is his rank ? He has the rank of colonel = Quel est son grade ? Il est colonel 
she's a lieutenant in the Army 
= elle est lieutenant dans l'armée de terre 
he's just a private 
 
 
= il est simple soldat 
Colonel Smith has arrived  
= le colonel Smith est arrivé 
 
L'anglais emploie l'article indéfini pour les noms de grades utilisés avec les verbes to be 
(être), to become (devenir), to make (faire) etc. 
 
Dans les expressions suivantes, « colonel » est pris comme exemple; les autres noms de 
grades s'utilisent de la même façon. 
 
il est colonel  
 
 
 
= 
he is a colonel 
il est colonel dans l'armée de terre 
= 
he is a colonel in the army 
devenir colonel 
 
 
 
= 
to become a colonel 
on l'a nommé colonel 
 
 
= 
he was made a colonel 
 
Mais avec le verbe « to promote » ou dans l'expression « the rank of … », l'anglais n'emploie 
pas l'article indéfini : 
 
être promu colonel  
= 
to be promoted colonel / to be promoted to colonel 
il a le grade de colonel 
= 
he has the rank of colonel 
 
L'anglais n'emploie pas non plus l'article défini lorsque le grade est suivi du nom propre : 
 
le colonel Jones est arrivé = 
Colonel Jones has arrived        [pas de « the »] 
le général Grunard   
=  
General Grunard 
 
Comparer : 
 
le colonel est arrivé  
= 
the colonel has arrived 
 
Noter que le mot « Colonel » prend une majuscule en anglais devant le nom propre, mais 
rarement dans les autres cas.

[Page 151 / 217 | chars: 1754]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Page 151 
 
2. Speaking to someone 
 
In the “armée de terre”, the “Mon” is short for “Monsieur” and is used to superior officers from 
lieutenant upwards, except for major. “Mon” is never prefixed to ranks in the marine nationale 
and never used to personnel of inferior rank in any of the three services, and to female 
personnel. 
 
Service personnel to superior officers : 
 
Yes, sir 
 
= 
oui, mon colonel (mon capitaine, mon lieutenant, etc.) 
Yes, sir !  
=  
à vos ordres ! 
Yes, ma'am 
= 
oui, colonel (capitaine, lieutenant, etc.) 
Reporting Sir ! / reporting for duty !  = Mes respects / à vos ordres mon colonel ! 
Good day to you, Sir ! (GB) / Good morning/afternoon, Sir ! (US) = Mes respects, mon 
capitaine/mon 
colonel ! 
 
 
Service personnel to someone of lower rank : 
 
yes, sergeant 
= 
oui, sergent 
  Address by rank (e.g.  “Gunnery Sergeant,” “Senior Chief”), not “sir/m’am.” 
 
 
En anglais comme en français, l'armée de terre et l'armée de l'air distinguent deux 
catégories : les officiers122, commissioned officers (GB) ou warrant officers (US), à partir du 
grade de Second Lieutenant / Pilot Officer, et tous les autres, à l'exception de 
Private/Aircraftman/Airman, non commissioned officers (the NCOs). 
 
 
Les abréviations sont utilisées uniquement par écrit et avec les noms propres, par ex.:  
 
Capt. Jones. 
 
 
S’adresser aux hommes = to address the men : 
“Soldats…!” = “Now men…!” 
“Bon travail, soldat…!” = “Good work, soldier…!” 
 
 
Monsieur l'ambassadeur / Madame l'ambassadrice (en s'adressant à lui/elle) = Ambassador 
 
‘pleased to meet you!’–‘likewise, I'm sure!’ = ‘enchanté!’—‘et moi de même!’ 
                                                          
122 Les officiers supérieurs = field officers.

À qui ai-je l’honneur ?

message ?

 Where can I reach Lieutenant Dark ? 
Vous pouvez le joindre au 340  
 You can reach him at 340. 
Son numéro de poste a changé, c’est le 312  
 his extension has changed to 312. 
Je suis navré(e), il est sur une autre ligne  
 I’m afraid he is on another line 
Est-ce qu’il peut me rappeler au … ?  
 Could he call me back on … ? 
Nous avons été coupés  
 We’ve been cut off 
Désolé de vous avoir dérangé  
 Sorry for disturbing 
Merci pour votre appel  
 Thank you for calling 
Merci beaucoup

Mais je vous en prie  
 You are very welcome (GB) 
Bonjour Mon…, c’est le Sergent H., comment allez-
vous ? 
Hi, this is SGT H., how are you doing today, Sir ?

J O I N I N G  L E T T E R  
 
 
From Commander J G C. Royal Navy 
 
 
 
ALLIED JOINT FORCE COMMAND HEADQUARTERS BRUNSSUM 
QUARTIER GÉNÉRAL DU COMMANDEMENT DES FORCES 
INTERARMÉES INTERALLIÉES À BRUNSSUM 
POST BOX 270 6440 AG BRUNSSUM 
THE NETHERLANDS 
TEL.: 31 (0) 45 526 3684 IVSN: 244 3684 
 
 
 
 
PO ………..(AWT) D M F. 
D232323Y 
PO’s MESS 
HMS CUMBERLAND 
BFPO 261 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
25 Apr 05   
 
 
I am writing to formally welcome you to the British and Naval Communities here in Brunssum, 
since we have now received confirmation of your draft to JFC Brunssum. The RN/RM presence 
here is currently 27- strong, headed up by Commander S TATE, as Senior RN Officer (SRNO). I 
am the current Divisional Officer for the Naval personnel within J3 Division but will be relieved by 
Commander C ROBINSON in June. You can contact me as above. Your “day job” here will be as 
the Maritime Functional Area Services (FAS) Applications Administrator, within OPS HQ/C2 
Management Section of the J3 (Current Operations) Division. The Maritime FAS used by NATO 
is MCCIS, which is similar to RNCSS. The training for this FAS takes place at the NATO CIS 
School in Latina, Italy. You will also be instrumental in providing the Recognised Maritime Picture 
and Common Operational Picture (COP) for the headquarters, whether it be static or deployed. 
Within the section you will also gain knowledge of the air and land FAS’s. Currently the section 
also provides assistance to the ISAF mission in Afghanistan, supports exercise and training 
planning and experimentation with the NATO Consultation, Command and Control Agency 
(NC3A) on future FAS systems under development. The section is multinational, consisting of 
two staff officers and 7 NCOs from Belgium, Canada, Turkey, France, Poland, Denmark and 
USA. 
The job will be challenging and busy and because of your previous experience on operations with 
the Royal Navy you will be looked upon by your peers within the section to provide good advice 
on picture management and procedures not just for maritime but joint operations as well. 
 
You should also be ready to deploy at short notice (possibly as little as 5 days notice to move) 
anywhere in the world to conduct NATO Operations. JFC Brunssum will be the NATO Response 
Force (NRF) lead HQ from July 06, and will conduct a major exercise in Cape Verde in which you 
will take part in as a member of the Combined Joint Task Force Headquarters staff (CJTF). To 
be deployable, there is a considerable amount of training and preparation to be undertaken. This 
can be achieved here in Brunssum, but I am sure that you will wish to get as far ahead of the 
game as you can before you arrive. The minimum standard of physical fitness is the RNFT. 
Additionally on arrival you will be checked for physical and dental well being at the British Medical 
Centre. Part of the medical preparation includes being up-to date for a range of vaccinations and 
inoculations including if you wish, undertaking the Anthrax Voluntary Immunisation Programme. 
Additionally you will also need to be in date for your APWT and AGR Testing. There are no 
current plans to deploy you on operations, but this can change at short notice when the HQ 
assumes the role of NRF Commander.

[Page 156 / 217 | chars: 2006]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Page 156 
We are very fortunate in the level of support available to call upon here in Brunssum. The UK 
Delegation and Support Unit (UKDSU) provides a well equipped British Medical Centre, a supply 
flight that provides not only Combat 95 etc but also a wide range of items from cash clothing. Our 
UPO is Tri-Service and provides excellent support. Within the UPO we have a hardworking RN 
element of POWTR Paulette K. and her sidekick WTR Matt B.. (PO K. has written to you 
separately). Finally the UKDSU provides access to education and resettlement, a library, Post 
Office, sport and AT, as well as administrating housing and accommodation for us all. 
 
I must warn you that there is a lot of legwork involved in your joining routine when you arrive: 
security procedures, ID cards, banks, driving licences, vehicle registering and so on. To guide 
you through it all, PO (AWT) Spider K. will be your Sponsor. His telephone number in the HQ is x 
3556. PO KELLY will in due course be in contact with you but do not hesitate to contact POWTR 
K. or myself if you have any questions before you arrive. Additionally I strongly recommend that 
you read the welcome pack that PO K. has sent to you and if you are able, access the UKDSU 
website: www.brunsummbrits.com. Likewise www.rnrefernce.mod.uk has a link to the 
overseas support unit (RNOSU), which gives good guidance for LFS drafts. 
 
From previous experience, you may well be aware of the challenges, but also the immense 
awards, of working in this multinational environment. NATO however is changing. The Alliance 
has undergone a transformation that is both speeding up and changing the focus of what we do. 
I am sure that it will not be possible, before you join, for you to attend the 1- Week NATO 
SNCOs’ Orientation Course at Oberammergau, Germany. However we shall endeavour to get 
you on this course from Brunssum: you will find it highly enlightening and beneficial. 
 
In the meantime, we look forward to meeting you in December.

L A  F I C H E  :  S S S  
 
STAFF SUMMARY SHEET (SSS) (JFCB 35 – 4) 
Subject: 
APP-11/ADATP-3 - GEOGRAPHIC POSITIONS IN MESSAGES 
Tasker Tracker reference :Tasker-1623 
 
1. 
Purpose:    To provide COS with a letter to answer Encl 1. 
2. 
Conclusion:    There are no inconsistencies within JFC Brunssum or ISAF regarding the reporting of 
 
geographic positions.  
3. 
Recommendation:    That COS approve and sign Encl 2. 
4. 
Background:    In Encl 1, SHAPE explained that a recent meeting pointed out inconsistencies in 
geographic reporting procedures in NATO publications.  
5. 
Discussion:   J2 Division sees no inconsistencies within JFC Brunssum or ISAF regarding the reporting 
of geographic positions.  Reporting procedures for the ISAF and NRF missions are promulgated in OPLANs 
30302 Rev 1 (ISAF) and 30463 (SFJW 06) in APPENDIX 1 TO ANNEX T - Geospatial Support: 
…the designated horizontal datum and grid for the [ISAF or MVFOR] operation are The World Geodetic 
System 1984 (WGS-84), and Universal Transverse Mercator (UTM) grid / Military Grid Reference 
System (MGRS) respectively. 
This statement is in accordance with BI-SC Directive 80-4 "Position Referencing in NATO" and STANAG 2211 
"Geodetic Datums, Projections, Grids and Grid References." This STANAG provides a detailed explanation of 
how to correctly conduct position referencing.  We have not found UTM or MGRS to be “erroneous and 
inadequate” as asserted in Encl 1. 
6. 
This Staff Summary Sheet has been fully coordinated with J3 
 
J. H. 
OF-5, USA F 
DH J2 
 
Date of Signature ___________

MINUTES (=DECISION SHEET) VOCAB 
 
On behalf of Colonel Joao Santos, Chief Operations Support Branch, the Chairman opened the meeting with a 
warm welcome to the attending delegates. 
The chairman opened the meeting by welcoming the group members. 
The chairman stated the aim/the purpose of the meeting. 
The chairman opened the briefing by a short review of Functional Area Systems (FAS) status within the DJTF HQ 
and stated the key focus of the group should be on the following points : … 
Group participants were then invited by the Chairman to introduce themselves. 
CC-Air Ramstein noted with concern that CAOC 1 perceived a possible capability gap in SSSB operations that may 
not allow the RAP from naval units to pass through into the Link-1 system.  
After further discussion and consultation with other CAOCs, it was determined that… 
CC-Air Ramstein also raised the issue of a lack of coordination for TDL coverage in the Baltic area.   
CC-Air Izmir provided a verbal update to ongoing TDL issues within the Izmir AOR.  
He reviewed the status of… 
The requirement for a training needs analysis was again emphasised by the Chairman. 
The Chairman reported to the group that 
CC-Air Ramstein briefed the group on the status of air policing in the Baltic States and its effect on TDLs.   
There was valuable discussion between many delegates on the clearing of JTIDS networks for use in national 
airspace. 
It was felt by the group that… 
With an outbrief the IPC work was summarized.  
Conclusion was that the goal of the conference was reached.  
Nations/commands are requested to confirm participation of assets preferably before MPC end Sep 05 and also 
look at other shortcomings and challenges identified.  
Chairman thanked all the representatives for their attendance and looks forward to working on resolving all the 
FAS issues prior to NRF FOC. 
 
 
Minutes Taking 
 
Sirs and Gentlemen, 
 
Let me just elaborate on the requirement here.  I am taking and writing up the minutes however, the value of this sort 
of conference is in the discussion which takes place inspired by the briefings.  To capture this discussion and record it 
is the task of the minute taker and to achieve that successfully rightly requires two people; the main minute taker and 
a back up who can take down key points and who is, ideally, more of a SME so that points are not missed through 
lack of understanding.  The minute taker (me) can then just pass the record of key points to the backup who can 
confirm that nothing has been missed.  This is hardly a difficult task and I am surprised at the debate it appears to be 
causing.  COS has had the position explained to him from the misinformation he was given last Friday at the COS 
mtg and agrees that this is sensible.  The requirement for a second minute taker therefore remains and I would be 
grateful for the names to be passed to J3 as requested below. 
 
I repeat, the main task remains with me and I will write the record.  The back up minute takers' task is to record the 
key points from his perspective and then agree with my record or add to it so that together the value of the discussion 
is captured.  The back up minute taker is only required during the topic for which he is the designated recorder. 
 
I trust that this fully explains to you the role and task of this function. 
 
Regards 
 
Martin 
 
M S P. 
CDR GBR N 
DOS Ch P&T 
x3435

Thanks 
 
WE THANK YOU ALL FOR THE PART YOU ARE PLAYING 
Thanks in advance for whatever assistance you may be able to offer. 
 
We are very thankful for the role of the United States in the unification of Germany and of Berlin, but also in the 
unification of Europe.  
 
we can be thankful for the actions of our soldiers and diplomats in responding so effectively to the brutal attacks 
on our country. We can be thankful as well for our NATO allies who have stood by our side in our common fight 
against terrorism. 
 
Let me start by expressing the gratitude of the Government of Sweden to Poland for its inititive to arrange this 
important seminar. It is also very gratifying to see that almost all EAPC countries are represented here in 
Warsaw.  
 
The support that SFOR offers to civilian implementation in addition to its primary task is an essential element in 
the continued achievement of progress.  
 
I also want to thank the civilian and military leaders of Bosnia and Herzegovina and members of the International 
Community for the warm welcome I have received. And, a special thanks to the soldiers on parade.  
Thank you again for being with us today. Thank you for your continued support of SFOR. 
 
Secretary General Lord Robertson, Admiral & Mrs. Venturoni, Ministers, Excellencies, Chiefs of Defense, 
Regional Commanders, fellow general and flag officers, distinguished guests, Mr. Mayor, ladies and gentlemen, 
comrades-in-arms:SecGen, thank you, for those kind remarks . . . and I would like to thank all of you for being 
here today. Your presence is a powerful symbol of the breadth of this alliance, and its significance to the Euro-
Atlantic community.The soldiers, sailors, airmen and marines on the field before us are also a symbol of this 
alliance's ultimate capabilities and strengths, founded on the profession of arms and representing a total of 44 
nations, as well as the subordinate commands of Allied Command Europe. Please join me in thanking them, and 
all the men and women serving today for NATO, who are doing the difficult and dangerous work in uniform for 
this alliance. 
 
To the men and women of Allied Command Europe, you have our deepest gratitude and respect for your 
support dedication and friendship, and our best wishes in the days and months ahead. We bid you all a fond 
farewell. 
 
Please accept my sincere thanks for your dedication and outstanding support during the recent NATO Air Meet 
2000 hosted by the Commander of HQ AIRNORTH, General Martin, in Karup, Denmark.  Your involvement 
ensured the highest level of service during the exercise and facilitated the adherence to our timetable.  Your 
efforts are greatly appreciated, and I am proud to have you on the HQ AIRNORTH Team!  
 
==== 
 
Thanks for the snapshot (= le point, le topo). 
It distills the brief very well keeping it simple for us knuckle draggers (hommes des cavernes). 
 
==== 
 
Thank you again for your support of FTEX and we look forward to continuing to work with you as we move 
ahead. 
 
==== 
 
Jose

Dear J., 
  
Back in Brussels, I would like to thank you for organizing a very instructive and interesting visit. It was an eye-
opener in many respects, in particular of course as regards our posture vis-a-vis Somalia, Puntland, and the 
PMPF. Djibouti, which so far I had just about located on the map, was also a fascinating experience. The 
inauguration party produced a strong impression that the mission is now a real team and ready to tackle the 
challenges ahead (or perhaps rather the remaining challenges after your difficult build-up). Please share my 
thanks with your team, in particular Natascha and Flavio, who seems to be a real asset for your mission. 
  
Best regards, 
  
H. 
  
PS I am only on a touch and go in my office to clear up the most necessary jobs and will then depart for my 
skiing holiday. We will talk over the telephone, as agreed, when I am back, during the week beginning 25 
February. 
Please note my new office telephone number: 
  
H. H.

L E T T E R  O F  R E F U S A L  
 
 
 
 
To : General xx 
Commander 
Component Command - xx 
 
 
_____ COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE 
 
Thank you for your letter of 2 Feb 07 outlining proposals for an expansion in the Common Operational Picture in 
____.  I agree that the maintenance of the highest possible level of Situational Awareness in an operational 
theatre is important.  However, there are considerations in this instance, which I believe are not completely 
conducive to a NATO-funded expansion programme. 
 
In consideration of the ____ operational necessity for the expansion of radar sensors in the North and West of 
____, our assessment of the current situation is that the degree of tactical air control requirements in the North 
and West is considerably lower than in the South and East.  Although the expansion of radar coverage within 
Regions North and West would certainly provide a great level of enhancement over the current situation, I 
assess that this currently falls above the threshold of what should be considered as the Minimum Military 
Requirement for conducting _____ operations in ____.   
 
Understandably, improving flight safety in ____ should always be a primary consideration and I appreciate your 
approach in suggesting enhancements.  Given the overall size of ____, we are breaking our assessment down 
by Regions and consider the potential overall value and benefit of radars in Regions North and West versus the 
overall ensuing cost.  Any additional input that you may wish to provide in order to inform our decision would be 
most welcome.  
 
I do not want this to seem as if I am closing the door on this issue, instead I offer you the opportunity to forward a 
detailed justification that would help us convince SHAPE as to why this requirement should be considered the 
Minimum Military Requirement.  Past experience has shown that highlighting the dangers of not including a 
capability vice listing its inherent benefits stands the greatest chance of success.   
 
I would also like to take the opportunity to mention that the NATO HQ-led Strategic Plan for the Normalisation of 
the ____ Aviation Sector in ____ deals with civil developments regarding additional radar sensors that may fit in 
the North and West of the country.  This is not NATO funded. However, if developed, it could also serve ____ 
purposes.

L E S  V Œ U X  :  X M A S  G R E E T I N G S  
 
SUBJ : CMC'S XMAS GREETINGS 
5 DECEMBER 2005 
 
CMC ANNUAL MESSAGE TO THE TROOPS 
 
FROM: THE CHAIRMAN OF THE NATO MILITARY COMMITTEE, GENERAL RAY HENAULT. 
 
SUBJECT: PERSONAL MESSAGE TO ALL NATO PERSONNEL DEPLOYED AWAY FROM HOME OVER THE 
HOLIDAY PERIOD - DECEMBER 2005 AND JANUARY 2006. 
 
ON BEHALF OF ALL MEMBERS OF THE NATO MILITARY COMMITTEE, I WOULD LIKE TO EXTEND OUR VERY 
BEST WISHES AND SEASONAL GREETINGS TO ALL WHO ARE DEPLOYED ON DUTY DURING THIS HOLIDAY 
PERIOD. 
 
YOU CAN ALL BE VERY PROUD OF THE ESSENTIAL ROLE YOU ARE PLAYING IN SAFEGUARDING THE VALUES 
AND FREEDOM OF THE ALLIANCE. YOUR CONTRIBUTION IS MORE IMPORTANT THAN EVER IN THE CURRENT 
SECURITY ENVIRONMENT, AND THE DEDICATION, JUDGEMENT AND PROFESSIONALISM THAT YOU DISPLAY 
IS A CREDIT TO ALL. 
WE ARE VERY AWARE OF THE DIFFICULT AND OFTEN DANGEROUS SITUATIONS THAT YOU FACE ON A 
DAILY BASIS, AND WE APPRECIATE WHAT YOU ARE DOING FOR YOUR COUNTRY AND FOR NATO. YOUR 
STRENGTH OF PURPOSE IS AN EXAMPLE TO US ALL, AND THE RESULTS THAT YOU ARE ACHIEVING ARE 
MAKING VERY REAL AND VERY POSITIVE DIFFERENCES TO THE LIVES OF SO MANY. 
NATO'S STRENGTH LIES IN ITS PEOPLE, ESPECIALLY THOSE LIKE YOURSELVES THAT ARE SERVING ON 
DEPLOYED OPERATIONS. BY DEFENDING AND PROMOTING SECURITY IN OPERATIONS AROUND THE 
WORLD OUR ALLIANCE IS MAKING A DIFFERENCE. BY HELPING LESS FORTUNATE NATIONS OVERCOME 
THEIR DIFFICULTIES AND REGAIN THEIR CONFIDENCE YOU ARE HELPING TO MAKE THE WORLD A BETTER 
PLACE, AND FOR THAT WE ARE GRATEFUL. 
OUR THOUGHTS ARE ALWAYS WITH YOU, BUT ESPECIALLY AT THIS TIME WHEN YOU ARE AWAY FROM 
YOUR HOMES AND YOUR FAMILIES. WE THANK YOU ALL FOR THE PART YOU ARE PLAYING AND SEND YOU 
OUR SINCERE BEST WISHES OVER THIS FESTIVE PERIOD AND FOR THE NEW YEAR. 
 
MESSAGE ANNUEL DU CMC AUX TROUPES DU PRESIDENT DU COMIT MILITAIRE DE L'OTAN, 
LE GENERAL RAY HENAULT 
 
OBJET : MESSAGE PERSONNEL TOUS LES MEMBRES DE L'OTAN DEPLOYES LOIN DE LEUR FOYER DURANT 
LA PERIODE DES FETES DE FIN D'ANNEE 2005/2006 
 
AU NOM DE TOUS LES MEMBRES DU COMITEE MILITAIRE DE L'OTAN, JE TIENS TRANSMETTRE TOUTES LES 
PERSONNES EN AFFECTATION MES MEILLEURS VOUX EN CETTE PERIODE DE FETES DE FIN D'ANNEE. 
VOUS POUVEZ TOUTES ET TOUS ETRE TRES FIERS DE VOTRE CONTRIBUTION ESSENTIELLE LA 
PROTECTION DES VALEURS ET DE LA LIBERTE DE L'ALLIANCE. VOTRE ROLE EST PLUS IMPORTANT QUE 
JAMAIS COMPTE TENU DU CONTEXTE DE SECURITE ACTUEL, ET LE 
DEVOUEMENT, LE JUGEMENT ET LE PROFESSIONNALISME DONT VOUS FAITES PREUVE SONT A VOTRE 
HONNEUR. 
 
NOUS SOMMES TOUT A FAIT CONSCIENTS DES SITUATIONS DIFFICILES ET SOUVENT DANGEREUSES QUE 
VOUS VIVEZ TOUS LES JOURS, ET NOUS APPRECIONS CE QUE VOUS FAITES POUR VOTRE PAYS ET POUR 
L'OTAN. VOTRE DETERMINATION EST EXEMPLAIRE, ET TOUT 
CE QUE VOUS ACCOMPLISSEZ A UNE INCIDENCE REELLE ET TRES POSITIVE SUR DE NOMBREUSES VIES. 
L'OTAN TIRE SA FORCE DE SON PERSONNEL, ET PARTICULIEREMENT DE GENS QUI, COMME VOUS, 
SERVENT DANS DES OPERATIONS DE DEPLOIEMENT. EN DEFENDANT ET EN ENCOURAGEANT LA SECURITE 
DANS LES OPERATIONS MENEES PARTOUT DANS LE MONDE, NOTRE ALLIANCE JOUE UN ROLE 
DETERMINANT. EN AIDANT LES PAYS MOINS FAVORISES A SURMONTER LEURS DIFFICULTES ET A 
RETROUVER LEUR CONFIANCE, VOUS CONTRIBUEZ A UN MONDE MEILLEUR, ET VOUS AVEZ TOUTE NOTRE 
GRATITUDE. 
NOS PENSEES VOUS ACCOMPAGNENT TOUJOURS, SURTOUT MAINTENANT QUE VOUS ETES LOIN DE CHEZ 
VOUS ET DE VOS FAMILLES. NOUS VOUS REMERCIONS POUR CE QUE VOUS FAITES, ET NOUS VOUS 
TRANSMETTONS NOS VOEUX LES PLUS SINCERES POUR NOEL ET LA NOUVELLE ANNEE. 
Vœux 
 
I would just like to take this opportunity to say many thanks to you all for your efforts and enthusiasm since my arrival 
in May.  J3 really is, in every way, the vital epicentre of JFC Brunssum.  Our ability to successfully hold that position is 
down to your efforts, ability and energy.  It is a huge privilege and pleasure to lead this team ! 
 
May I also take this opportunity to wish you and your families a great Xmas and a happy and successful New Year. 
I look forward to seeing you in 2008. 
 
Graham S. 
G E S. 
Air Cdre 
J3 Division Head, Ext 2620

Sirs, Ladies, Gentlemen, 
 
Please find attached the updated SOP for CJOC evacuation.   
 
The bottom line is that if you hear the evacuation alarm - you get out quickly and safely, via the nearest safe exit 
point.  Evacuation includes everyone, whether exercise or for real. 
 
If you have any questions regarding the information above, please do not hesitate to contact me.

Excuses/Apology 
Correction to CJOC Briefing and an apology from POLAD 
 
Colleagues, 
During the POLAD briefing on 4 Mar, I improperly read a headline from a news media article that referred to 
"Macedonia".   
 
The official NATO policy on this issue is clear.  The only appropriate terminology to be used within NATO is "the 
former Yugoslav Republic of Macedonia" which may by abbreviated as "FYROM".  (STANAG 1050) 
I sincerely apologize for giving offence to anyone who may have heard the briefing or been informed about it.  
This error was mine and does not in any way reflect any change of NATO policy on this issue, nor any intent on 
my part to express an opinion on the issue.  I regret any unhappiness this error may have caused. 
Jack S. (A6 - USA) 
Chief POLAD

layout, I think we all realize that there are a few areas which could be improved.  You are invited to provide your 
inputs directly to me so that I can collate them and provide a consolidated response.

Please be realistic (it is unlikely that extreme reconstruction is likely). 
Consider the impact of the changes (both positive and negative). 
Bare in mind that it is unlikely that any of your suggestions will be realized in the immediate future. 
Use the slide provided and annotate your comments etc. 
Remember that this is for discussion purposes; the finite detail is not necessary. 
Provide all response by 12:00 on 5th April (to enable time to collate, discuss etc)

Organizing workshops 
 
Ladies and Gentlemen, 
 
Based on the outstanding results from the Operational Working Group that was held in conjunction with our 
Force Tracking Experiment (FTEX) at NC3A in February, and the ongoing work (and initial successes) with 
NATO-Nation Interoperability in ISAF, ACT will be conducting a follow-on Friendly Force Tracking (FFT) 
Interoperability Workshop. It will be held at JFC Brunssum in the Amsterdam Room 10 - 12 October 2007. Many 
thanks go to JFC Brunssum for agreeing to host this expectedly large gathering. 
 
The agenda is still in work, but the highlighted topics are: 
NATO-Nation Interoperability (NNI) in ISAF, including Lessons Learned 
NATO Friendly Force Information (NFFI) data standard 
Land Track Interoperability Service (LTIS) for future NATO operations 
FFT and Coalition Combat Identification (CCID) 
FFT and Air Command and Control 
National FFT Capabilities and plans for NNI 
NATO FFT Capability Development 
 
The calling notice (attached) is being sent to ACO and the National Liaison Representatives here at ACT. We 
wanted to make sure that all of you get it as soon as possible.  
 
CDR M. of the NATO HQ C3 Staff is requested to distribute to the C3 Reps.  
ACO is requested to distribute to your subordinate commands as you see fit. 
 
 
Also attached is an electronic version of the Registration Form to make it easier for you to register. Deadline for 
registration is 23 September, but since hotels in Brunssum are limited, we suggest that you book your rooms 
early.

Demande de salle de conférence 
 
Marcel, 
As I mentioned to you on the phone yesterday, we are looking to have another FFT Interoperability Workshop 
sometime in the fall.  Our preferred dates are 26 - 28 September or 10 - 12 October.  Waiting until the fall will 
allow us to address the operational issues that come out of implementing interoperability in ISAF and review the 
STANAG process - similar to what we did during the FTEX.   
The issue we are having is finding a location.  As this workshop will have some direct impact on operations in 
ISAF, would it be possible for JFC - Brunssum to host during either one of those sets of dates? 
Thanks, 
Joe
