 
Use proper memo format in all correspondence. 
 
Address the officer by title and last name (e.g.  “LT Smith,”) or sir/ma’am (e.g.  “Good afternoon sir,”) in the first 
line of the e-mail. 
 
Sir/ma’am does not need to be written after sentences in the e-mail as it would be used verbally; write as you 
would in a normal business e-mail. 
 
Close the e-mail with “Very respectfully, (skip a line) MIDN class last name.” 
 
Source : http://navyrotc.berkeley.edu/resources/MIDN%20Guidebook/5%20Military%20Section.htm 
 
 
 
Veuillez trouver ci-dessus = please find above 
 
Veuillez trouver ci-dessous = please find below / hereunder 
 
Veuillez trouver ci-joint = please find attached / enclosed 
 
Je vous transfère les renseignements suivants = FYI (Forward You Information) 
 
(Message pour tous) A tous = Alcon (To all those who are connected)

Réponse à une invitation : acceptation 
 
Dear Mrs Mayhew, 
It is very good of you to invite me to dinner and I shall be delighted to come on July 4th.

I am settled in London in order to confirm the arrangements.

Yours sincerely, 
Sophie Beauverie 
 
 
 
Réponse à une invitation : refus 
 
Dear Dr Soames, 
Thank you for your kind invitation to dinner on the 19th. Unfortunately, my plans have changed 
somewhat, and I am leaving England earlier than I had expected in order to attend a literary 
conference in New York. I am sorry to miss you, but perhaps I could call you next time I am in England, 
and we could arrange to meet. 
Until then, kindest regards, 
Michael Strong

tell. 
In the hurry of packing to leave, I seem to have picked up a red and white striped T-shirt. If you let me 
know where to send it, I'll put it in the post at once. My apologies. 
Many thanks once again. 
Yours, 
Julia (Robertson)

Page 191 
 
 
Note No. TXS 027/02/11 
 
 
Protocol Directorate of the Foreign and Commonwealth Office presents its compliments to all 
Diplomatic Missions in London and has the honour to remind them of the procedures for 
security arrangements for VIP visitors to the United Kingdom. 
 
Diplomatic & VIP Security Section in Protocol Directorate is the first point of contact for all 
security matters relating to a VIP visit, including requests for protection.  It is essential that 
Missions give as much information as possible in good time, providing arrival and departure 
details, accommodation and the visit programme.  Details of any accompanying officials and 
security officers should also be provided.   This information must be sent to Diplomatic & VIP 
Security Section immediately (e-mail: ProtocolSecurity@fco.gov.uk or direct fax: 020 7008 
1026). 
 
Diplomatic & VIP Security Section is the contact point for radio frequency requests.  To clear 
a radio frequency for use during a VIP visit, the following information must be provided: 
bandwidth, frequency range (spectrum), preferred frequency and alternative, area of usage 
and power output.  
Missions are reminded that the import and use of electronic counter measures (ECM) or 
similar devices to jam radio frequencies is not permitted. 
 
Missions are also reminded that under United Kingdom law, the carrying of firearms, batons, 
Pepper and CS spray, tasers and knives by security officers is prohibited.  Any weapons 
brought into the United Kingdom must be surrendered to the appropriate United Kingdom 
port authorities on arrival and weapons will be securely stored.  Diplomatic & VIP Security 
Section must be given details of all weapons being brought into the United Kingdom so that 
the relevant port authorities can be notified in advance of the visit. Missions must also notify 
airlines or private charter companies, before departure, that weapons will be taken onboard 
the aircraft.   
 
Delay in providing the above information could hinder the Foreign and Commonwealth Office 
in meeting the Mission’s request for assistance.  It is Protocol Directorate's responsibility to 
liaise with the Metropolitan Police and other Agencies who may be involved security for the 
visit. 
 
Protocol Directorate avails itself of this opportunity to renew to all Diplomatic Missions in 
London the assurance of its highest consideration. 
 
 
 
 
FOREIGN AND COMMONWEALTH OFFICE 
LONDON SW1 
 
9 March 2011

Page 192 
 
The Latin phrase ad interim (abbr. ad int, literally "in the time between") means "in the meantime" or 
"temporarily". A diplomatic officer who acts in place of an ambassador is called chargé d'affaires ad interim or 
chargé d’affaires a.i.

11.  EXPRÉSSIONS ET LOCUTIONS POUR LA 
DISCUSSION 
 
 
 
 
L ’ I N T R O D U C T I O N  
Ce texte nous informe sur …  
This text acquaints us with … 
Ce texte est une analyse exacte de la situation  
This text is an accurate analysis of the situation. 
Ceci est dû essentiellement à trois facteurs  
This is basically due to three factors. 
Le point controversé est … 
La question qui pose problème est … 
The point / the question at issue is that … 
L’essentiel du problème, c’est …  
The core of the problem is this : 
La question se résume à ceci… 
What this question boils down to is … 
De quoi s’agit-il ? 
What is the main point / main question ? 
J’aimerais particulièrement attirer votre attention sur 
… 
I would particularly like to turn your attention to … 
I would particularly like to draw your attention to 
En guise d’introduction … 
By way of introduction … 
Je me contenterai d’esquisser … 
I will confine myself to a brief outline of … 
J’ai divisé mon sujet en trois parties. Premièrement…  I’ve divided my topic into three parts. First… 
J’ai divisé mon sujet comme suit…  
I’ve divided my topic as follows… 
Pour couvrir le sujet de façon adéquate, je propose de 
diviser ma présentation en deux parties  
To cover the subject adequately I propose to divide my 
presentation into two parts 
Je commencerai par…, je passerai ensuite à…, enfin 
je décrirai…  
I’ll start with…, then I’ll move to…, finally I’ll describe…

[Page 202 / 217 | chars: 3141]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Page 202 
L E S  T R A N S I T I O N S  
En outre, par ailleurs  
furthermore, moreover 
Passons maintenant à la Région Ouest  
Now let’s move on to Region West… 
Coming to Region West… 
Avant de passer à…, j’aimerais parler quelques 
minutes de…  
Before I move on to …, I’d like to spend a few minutes 
on … 
Je vais maintenant vous donner un aperçu de la 
situation actuelle dans la Région Nord  
now I’m going to give you an insight into the current 
situation in Region North 
Ceci m’amène à …  
this brings me to  the subject of…  
 
to deal with… 
 
L E  D É V E L O P P E M E N T  
En ce qui concerne  
On plans…/ As regards… 
A l’exception de…  
But for…/ Except… 
Nul ne conteste…  
Nobody contests… 
Ce qui est en jeu, c’est…  
What is at stake is… 
Après une visite à Bruxelles…  
After a visit to Brussels… 
Cette fois,…  
For once…/ This time… 
Au fur et à mesure que les événements se 
déroulaient…  
As events unfolded… 
Le(s) premier(s) suggérai(en)t…, le(s) second(s) 
proposai(en)t…  
The former suggested…, the latter proposed… 
En vertu de cet accord…  
Under this agreement… 
A cet effet…  
To this end… / To this effect… 
En matière de  
On matters pertaining to (= about) 
Aussi étrange/surprenant que cela puisse paraître…  
Strange/Surprising as/though it may seem/sound … 
Quoi qu’il arrive…  
Come what may… (= Whatever may happen) 
Il serait mal venu de …  
It would be churlish to… 
Certains… Inversement, d’autres…  
Some people… Conversely, others… 
Il ne s’agit pas à proprement parler de …, mais de…  
It is not a question of …, but rather of… 
Tout semble effectivement indiquer que …  
Everything certainly seems to point to … 
Nous ne pouvons que nous incliner devant ces 
conclusions  
We130 can only bow to these conclusions.

12.  LES FAUX-AMIS 
 
 
 
 
Ce sont des mots qui ont une orthographe proche ou identique au français mais dont le sens est souvent le 
contraire de ce que l’on pense. 
On peut les ranger dans 2 catégories : ceux qui ont un sens inattendu, et ceux qui combinent un sens inattendu 
et un sens prévisible. 
  
Ex : acceptable 
1) agréable, qui fait plaisir. 
2) acceptable. 
  
LES SURPRENANTS

accomplir, réaliser.

« Commiseration ! » : said by COL NEWMAN, following Ireland’s victory over France (rugby game).

LES PREVISIBLES 
  
Les mots qui se trouvent dans cette catégorie ont deux sens. LE SENS LE PLUS LOGIQUE EST LE PLUS 
RARE. 
  
acceptable (adj) 
 
agréable, bienvenu, qui fait plaisir ; acceptable. 
to accomodate 
 
loger, accueillir, recevoir ; s’accommoder. 
anxious (adj)  
 
tenir beaucoup à faire qqch. 
anxiety  
 
 
désir, impatience ; anxiété. 
an apology 
 
 
une excuse, une justification; une apologie 
to apologize 
 
 
s’excuser, regretter.

un appareil photo; une camera de cinema. 
comfort 
 
 
la consolation, le réconfort ; le confort.

a crab  
 
 
morpion ; pomme sauvage ; crabe. 
to cry  
 
 
pleurer ; pousser un cri, des cris. 
  
a date  
 
 
un rendez-vous, un « rancard »; une datte ; une date. 
a delay  
 
 
un retard ; un délai. 
a demonstration 
 
« une manif » ; une démonstration. 
a detail 
un détachement militaire ; des coordonnées personnelles ; un détail.

to dispose of

13.  COMMON MILITARY PHRASES 
 
Toutes ces expressions sont d’origine militaire et couramment employées dans le langage courant. 
 
 
 
up in arms: signifie indigné, furieux.  
Ex : The local inhabitants are up in arms over the proposed new motorway.   
to backfire : s’utilise pour dire que qqch se déroule mal en entraînant des conséquences fâcheuses pour ceux 
qui en sont en responsables.  
Ex : Their plan backfired on them.   
that’s half the battle :c’est déjà à moitié gagné. S’utilise pour indiquer qu’une attitude particulière constitue la 
bonne démarche vers le succès.  
Ex : In a difficult law suit, if you can find a good lawyer, that’s half the battle.   
to fight a losing battle : s’utilise quand l’on se trouve dans une situation que l’on ne pourra pas résoudre, que 
les chances sont inégales.  
Ex : He is fighting a losing battle in the courts in order to prove that he is innocent.  
 
die-hard : intransigeant, réactionnaire.  
Ex : He is well-known for his die-hard views.   
to hold the fort : s’utilise pour dire que qqun se retrouve seul à assumer des responsabilités habituellement 
partagées avec d’autres.  
Ex : The manager is holding the fort because most staff members are absent or busy.   
to stick to one’s guns : maintenir sa position, ne pas changer d’avis.  
Ex : He sticks to his guns and insists that he is telling the truth.   
to cause havoc : causer des dégâts, du désordre.  
Ex :The storm caused havoc in many  areas.  
loopholes : vides (surtout juridique) dans les lois qui permettent de les contourner.  
Ex : The new law contains loopholes which will enable people to evade it.   
Tell it to the marines ! : expression qui exprime une grande incrédulité.  
Ex : You must be joking. Tell it to the marines !   
 
point-blank: à bout portant137 ( ballistique). S’utilise figurativement pour dire que qqch est fait de façon directe et 
abrupte, une question par exemple.  
Ex : He asked me point-blank to lend him a large sum of money.   
to close ranks : s’utilise pour parler d’un besoin accru de coopération en cas de menace ou de défi commun.  
Ex : The profession has closed ranks in order to press for better pay and conditions.   
to have a shot at : essayer d’accomplir qqch.  
Ex : I may not be successful, but at least I’m determined to have a shot at it.   
smokescreen : écran de fumée. S’utilise figurativement en parlant d’une manœuvre tactique visant à égarer 
qqun pour lui cacher ce que l’on fait ou ce que l’on veut cacher.  
Ex : The statement is simply a smokescreen to mask their real ambitions.  
 
 
Source : École des Fourriers de Querqueville, janvier 2005. 
 
EV Edme / EV1 Waronski / EV Michel 
Ecole des fourriers de Querqueville - BP 30 - 50115 CHERBOURG ARMEES 
 
 
 
                                                          
137 Elle sortit une arme de sous ses vêtments, et tira à bout portant = she produced a gun from beneath her clothes and shot point blank.
